# Sweep #6 · Terminal 19 — THE ADMIN SERVER ROUTES, PART A

**Territory:** `app/api/admin/**` — the first 25 `route.ts` files in alphabetical order
(`find app/api/admin -name route.ts | sort | head -25`).
**Phase block:** P09001–P09500 (permanent — never renumber).
**Branch:** `sweep6/t19-admin-api-a` · **worktree:** `sweep6/T19` · **port:** 4119.

## How the 500 are budgeted

| block | ids | phases | share | what it is |
|-------|-----|--------|-------|------------|
| A | P09001–P09025 | 25 | 5% | the sign-in gate, one row per file (conformance) |
| B | P09026–P09100 | 75 | 15% | conformance to the written project rules (columns · bounds · plain words) |
| C | P09101–P09300 | 200 | 40% | reading the code for correctness — 8 rows per file |
| D | P09301–P09375 | 75 | 15% | watching it run (headless on port 4119, admin cookie, zero sign-ins) |
| E | P09376–P09425 | 50 | 10% | screenshots actually opened and read (1280×800 **and** A35 360×780 dpr3) |
| F | P09426–P09475 | 50 | 10% | tracing a change across panels |
| G | P09476–P09500 | 25 | 5% | my own judgment about whether a real restaurant needs it this way |

Conformance total = A + B = 100 rows (20%). Every one of the 25 files owned here has **at least
ten** rows across A–C; the routes the scope names by hand have more.

**How to re-run this next sweep:** `node scripts/verify-admin-api-a.mjs` covers blocks A and B
outright. Blocks C–G are re-run by following the "how to verify" column; every live row uses
`adminHeaders()` from `scripts/sweep/login.mjs`, which makes **zero** sign-in requests.

# SWEEP #7 · Terminal 19 — the second pass over the same ground

**Re-run first, in place: all 500 rows of `P09001`–`P09500`.** Result: **498 ✅ · 2 ⏭ · 0 ❌ — no
regression.** Eleven rows had their expectation MOVED (the product changed: the recycle bin can now be
looked inside, the bill trail is paged, and the 90-day wait before a permanent removal was deleted by
the owner on 2026-08-20), and **four rows had the wrong text in their note column** — sweep #6 pasted
its FAILURE sentence beside a passing result on `P09408`, `P09413`, `P09418` and `P09425`. All four were
re-driven live and all four genuinely pass; the notes now say what was observed.

One row looked like a regression and was not: `P09438` (the view-as cookie riding on the quick-open
redirect). My own check grepped `lfh_admin_act`; the cookie is **`aevidine_admin_rid`**. Read with
`getSetCookie()` it is there, on both doors. **The detector was wrong, not the product** — the third
time this ledger has recorded that shape.

**Re-run date matters here:** `P09001`–`P09500` were re-run against the code as it stood on **2026-08-27**. `main` moved 591 commits between then and 2026-09-01, so the SECOND 500 below (`P53201`–`P53700`) is the pass that covers this boundary as it is today — and it re-derived all thirteen of this branch's fixes against the moved code before re-applying them.

**New block: `P24101`–`P24600`** (500), plus `P15025`–`P15027` taken from the ID REPAIR BLOCK for item 13 (written as `P15019`–`P15021`, then MOVED: T27 had renumbered its own four rows into `P15019`–`P15022` on 2026-08-28 and reached `main` first, so the terminal still in a branch is the one that moves — this file's own rule) — a fault that landed upstream while this pass was running and was caught by the guard on the rebase (500), written after reading all 3,612 lines of the 26 route files
rather than from outside them.

**Territory drift worth knowing:** `find app/api/admin -name route.ts | sort | head -25` now ends at
`printing/[...path]`, which pushed `rate-limits` to position 26 — into no territory's range at all.
Its ledger rows are T19's, so this pass covers **26 files**: the 25 in the roadmap plus `rate-limits`.
That drift had also switched three of the guard's four rules off for that file, silently (item 7).

| block | ids | rows | what it is |
|-------|-----|------|------------|
| A | P24101–P24126 | 26 | the guard covers this file BY NAME, so coverage cannot narrow by sorting |
| B | P24127–P24177 | 51 | the unchecked-read audit — every read site judged, one row each, harm named |
| C | P24178–P24257 | 80 | code-reading correctness on ground the old ids do not cover |
| D | P24258–P24323 | 66 | watching it run (port 4219, admin cookie, zero sign-ins) |
| E | P24324–P24383 | 60 | the fifteen screens, desktop and A35, screenshots opened and read |
| F | P24384–P24448 | 65 | the write paths and cross-panel truth, every row cleaned up by its own id |
| G | P24449–P24503 | 55 | the twelve fixes, each verified after the change |
| H | P24504–P24600 | 97 | the project rules that govern this area, my own judgment, and the handoffs |

**How to re-run this next sweep:** `node scripts/verify-admin-api-a.mjs` covers block A and the four
rules outright, and now prints its own coverage. Blocks B–H are re-run by following the *how to verify*
column; every live row uses `adminHeaders()` from `scripts/sweep/login.mjs`, which makes **zero**
sign-in requests, and every write row seats its own fixture on table `T19-BILL` and retires it.

| id | check | how to verify | result | note |
|----|-------|---------------|--------|------|
| P24101 | /api/admin/act-as/go is named in verify:admin-api-a's PART_A list, so rules 2-4 cannot stop covering it because a new route sorts earlier | node scripts/verify-admin-api-a.mjs — PART_A is an explicit list of 26 paths; a route in neither PART_A nor PART_B_NOT_YET fails the guard | ✅ | the quick-open door — green. This is the check that did not exist when printing/[...path] took position 25 and pushed rate-limits out of rules 2-4 (item 7) |

# SWEEP #7 · Terminal 19 — THE SECOND 500: the whole boundary, planned from the inside

**Written 2026-09-01, at the owner's instruction: *"plan whole 500 phases test within your
boundaries and make sure it cover all the thing within boundaries and do the test and fix the
things"*.** So this block is not a sample. It is an INVENTORY of the territory, counted from the
code, with one check per thing counted:

| what was counted | how | rows |
|---|---|---|
| exported handlers | `export async function GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE` across the 26 files | **39** |
| database calls | every `sb.from(...)` / `sb.rpc(...)` | **201** |
| parameters and branches | query parameters, body actions, path segments, body flags | **87** |
| refusals that are not the sign-in gate | every `status: 4xx/5xx` | **61** |
| the reply of every file | is every number read or honestly absent · does the money rule hold | **52** |
| the live drive | 153 real calls on port 4219, zero sign-ins | **50** |
| the project's rules for this area, and my own read | | **10** |
| | | **500** |

**26 files, 4,531 lines, 201 database calls — every one of them has a row here.** The territory is
the first 25 of `find app/api/admin -name route.ts | sort`, plus `rate-limits`, which the alphabet
pushed to position 26 and out of every territory's range.

**What it found: 8 faults, all fixed on this branch** — five from reading the reads (items 18–21),
one from turning the guard's own coverage back on (item 7), one from driving a parameter nobody had
driven (item 22 — `?day=2026-13-45` answered 500), and one from the boundary's own screens
(item 1). The other 492 rows are green, and "this part is clean" is most of the answer.

**Two things worth keeping for the next sweep:**
- **A static pass over every query is worth writing before reading the files.** 201 queries, 49
  flagged for a human read, 5 genuine faults, 44 documented tolerances. Reading 201 statements
  cold would have found fewer and taken longer.
- **Driving a parameter beats reading it.** `?day=2026-13-45` passes a `\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}`
  regular expression, and the comment above that line claimed the value was "validated to a strict
  date". Only a real call showed the 500.
